# EXPANSION CW125-07 — We Will Not Forget

## A prose-first memorial expansion about the remembered claim, the people who inherit it, and the blanks that remain human.

### Prose Wave 125: What a Warning Cannot Finish

## Batch brief

**Content type:** original game-content proposal with full scene, attributable writing, conversation, and conditional-return drafts.
**Content bank:** 48 beat pairings; four alternative authored forms for each pairing; 192 candidates, of which a verified consumer should select only what it can support.
**Current local anchor:** memorial_meridian_compact/not_forget — Meridian Compact Ally.
**Tone:** A small story around a memorial naming an ally from the fictional Meridian Compact, and what gratitude can say without defining an entire community or alliance.
**Scope:** narrative prose only; no production code, authoritative JSON edits, mechanics, route, quest, flag, simulation, or save change.

## 1. Expansion thesis

This plan explores A small story around a memorial naming an ally from the fictional Meridian Compact, and what gratitude can say without defining an entire community or alliance. It uses the source text “A friend from the Meridian Compact who stood with us in our darkest hour. Their alliance brought us hope when we had none. We will not forget.” as a narrow canon anchor, not as an excuse to manufacture witnesses or explain every silence. Six movements and forty-eight beat pairings explore how different present readers may respond to the wording. The proposed passages remain optional editorial drafts until a current owner and route are verified.

## 2. Story question

How can gratitude for one remembered ally remain personal and credible without inventing a treaty, faction doctrine, political history, or universal relationship between communities?

## 5. Human center

A public “we” trying to thank a friend whose own voice and reasons are absent from the catalog entry.
 The deceased or collective subject must never become a device whose only purpose is to teach a clean lesson to the living. Keep the person or people larger than the inscription’s most repeatable line.

## 6. Voice and point of view

- **A person speaking for the memorial’s “we”:** Knows the gratitude belongs to the inscription and cannot speak for every survivor.
- **A listener from another community:** Wants to honor a friend without claiming to know their motives.
- **A careful archivist:** Keeps the distinction between one ally and an entire faction visible.

The people listed here are proposed present-day readers, not canon characters and not witnesses unless another verified source establishes that history. Keep the original memorial voice distinct from any reply written for this plan.

## 7. Placement and current reachability

The exact local anchor is memorial_meridian_compact/not_forget. MemorialSystem owns survivor memorialization, grief, mourning, and eulogies, but this separate data record’s loader and player-facing consumer have not been verified. Before content placement, confirm the current schema, owner, route, and presentation context. Catalog presence alone proves no playable route. This plan contributes no new route or content registry.

## 8. Player agency

The player may read, ask who authored a claim, hear a second interpretation, decline to discuss it, or leave without a verdict. These are editorial postures rather than promised controls. Silence must remain a complete response. Do not attach approval, trust, reputation, item, resource, treatment, memorial completion, or relationship state to the reader’s interpretation.

## 9. Continuity, dignity, and safety

Meridian Compact is a fictional group named only here. Do not invent a treaty, leader, member roster, policy, territory, military role, event chronology, or relationship mechanics. The memorial establishes one ally as remembered by its writer, not the position of every member or community.
 Keep the source statement, a proposed speaker’s interpretation, and any verified world fact visibly separate. Avoid reenacting death, assigning fault without evidence, or converting grief into a tutorial.

## 11. Narrative sequence

These six editorial movements organize the prose bank; they are not six required visits, a chronology, or a quest chain. A verified consumer may use a single beat, a short excerpt, or none of the candidates.

### Movement 1: A Friend, Not a Category

The inscription begins with an individual relationship before naming a group. In this movement the candidate question is: What does “friend” mean when only one side is represented? The memorial’s phrase “a friend” gives the reader a concrete point of attention, while the record remains a public statement rather than a complete witness account. The scene should let people disagree about meaning without asking the player to approve one interpretation.

### Movement 2: From the Meridian Compact

The affiliation gives context but not a full faction profile. In this movement the candidate question is: How can a group name remain background rather than become doctrine? The memorial’s phrase “Meridian Compact” gives the reader a concrete point of attention, while the record remains a public statement rather than a complete witness account. The scene should let people disagree about meaning without asking the player to approve one interpretation.

### Movement 3: Stood with Us

A phrase of companionship leaves the action unspecified. In this movement the candidate question is: What can solidarity mean without an invented scene? The memorial’s phrase “stood with us” gives the reader a concrete point of attention, while the record remains a public statement rather than a complete witness account. The scene should let people disagree about meaning without asking the player to approve one interpretation.

### Movement 4: Our Darkest Hour

The inscription gives the remembered moment a public scale. In this movement the candidate question is: Who is the “us” that calls this hour darkest? The memorial’s phrase “our darkest hour” gives the reader a concrete point of attention, while the record remains a public statement rather than a complete witness account. The scene should let people disagree about meaning without asking the player to approve one interpretation.

### Movement 5: Alliance Brought Hope

A collective noun and an emotion share one sentence. In this movement the candidate question is: Can gratitude be expressed without deciding what an alliance must be? The memorial’s phrase “their alliance” gives the reader a concrete point of attention, while the record remains a public statement rather than a complete witness account. The scene should let people disagree about meaning without asking the player to approve one interpretation.

### Movement 6: We Will Not Forget

The promise belongs to a speaking “we,” not a verified universal record. In this movement the candidate question is: What does remembrance owe the absent friend? The memorial’s phrase “brought us hope” gives the reader a concrete point of attention, while the record remains a public statement rather than a complete witness account. The scene should let people disagree about meaning without asking the player to approve one interpretation.

## 12. Beat bank and alternative passage drafts

Every movement meets each of eight source phrases. The four forms per pairing are alternatives: an observable scene, an attributable reader-authored fragment, a two-voice conversation, and a conditional return. Their headings help writers compare form; they do not promise that all versions fit one consumer. Dialogue is deliberately from proposed present readers, not from the person memorialized.

### Beat 01: A Friend, Not a Category × a friend

**Beat question:** When a reader meets “a friend” during “A Friend, Not a Category,” what can they honor in that wording while remembering that an intimate word that does not supply the absent person’s identity?

#### Scene draft 001 — a friend — A Friend, Not a Category

This version starts in the small interval between recognizing the record and deciding what to repeat from it. The dramatic work of “A Friend, Not a Category” comes from placing “a friend” beside what the memorial does not say. No invented detail is allowed to close that gap. The proposed reader is not a witness to the life being memorialized; their presence only gives the source wording a present audience.

Let the focus settle on “a friend”. The phrase has force because an intimate word that does not supply the absent person’s identity. A second person notices that force before anyone turns it into an explanation. They may look toward the line, then toward the person who has stopped reading, giving the beat a human action rather than an invented flashback.

A visitor mindful of the friend’s absent perspective begins with a modest response: “I can read a friend, but I cannot tell from this sentence everything that happened. I want to keep the wording because it matters to someone, and I want to leave room where the record leaves room.” The line is written as present interpretation, not a quotation falsely attributed to the person on the memorial.

A keeper who refuses to turn one remembrance into faction canon replies, “Then let us ask what it asks of us now. When a reader meets “a friend” during “A Friend, Not a Category,” what can they honor in that wording while remembering that an intimate word that does not supply the absent person’s identity? I can honor the claim and still notice what it does not tell us.” The answer should be specific to the source phrase; it must not flatten the disagreement into a lesson about all grief or all survivors.

A third voice, a memorial reader who is grateful but precise about what they know, may interrupt gently. They do not correct the catalog or add evidence. They ask whether the word “a friend” is being repeated because it comforts the room, because it warns the room, or because no one knows what else to say. Let more than one motive remain possible.

The scene’s emotional turn around “a friend” in “A Friend, Not a Category” is small: one listener stops trying to finish the missing story for a moment. A chair shifts, a copied line is turned face down and then face up again, or the speaker chooses a less certain verb. The selected gesture should fit the verified presentation context; no physical memorial site is established by this proposal.

For “a friend” in “A Friend, Not a Category,” player-facing tone stays open. The player may read, ask for the source wording again, decline the conversation, or simply listen. These are writing possibilities, not a promised menu or branching system. No answer earns approval, trust, resources, safety, reputation, or a new relationship state.

The closing breath belongs to the living reader; do not fabricate an answer on behalf of the person commemorated. The last attention returns to “a friend”, now heard through the live disagreement rather than treated as a complete biography.

**Drafting focus.** Keep the reader’s attention on “a friend” rather than on a newly invented biography. The writer may ask When a reader meets “a friend” during “A Friend, Not a Category,” what can they honor in that wording while remembering that an intimate word that does not supply the absent person’s identity? The answer should emerge through voice, hesitation, and what the speakers decline to claim; it should not be delivered by an omniscient narrator. This restraint is part of the content, not a substitute for dramatic movement.

**Voice and performance.** Let a visitor mindful of the friend’s absent perspective carry a rhythm distinct from the other proposed speakers. For this “A Friend, Not a Category” beat on “a friend,” that person can pause, qualify a sentence, or disagree with a word. They cannot borrow the deceased person’s interior voice or pretend that a catalog label gives them access to a private memory. Shape the delivery so the audience can hear the difference between a quoted source fact and an editorial response.

## 13. Player-facing tone

Keep sentences close to the source wording and the present reader’s actual knowledge. Allow one voice to value the memorial and another to object to its universal-sounding conclusion. Neither reaction should be framed as the correct morality. Use quiet material action rather than explanatory narration. Avoid turning illness, hunger, conflict, radiation, cold, machinery, duty, or trust into a puzzle tutorial or instruction to the player.

## 14. Editorial acceptance

Meridian Compact is a fictional group named only here. Do not invent a treaty, leader, member roster, policy, territory, military role, event chronology, or relationship mechanics. The memorial establishes one ally as remembered by its writer, not the position of every member or community.
 Re-check the exact local record and any newer narrative data before selecting text. Retain only passages that the current consumer can reach and attribute honestly. Shortening a candidate is preferable to implying unsupported canon.

## 15. Character and relationship continuity

The proposed roles are anonymous present-day readers. Do not promote them into named survivors, historical witnesses, patients, faction leaders, victims, or permanent companions without a separate source and authorized content decision. Their relationships exist only within the selected text. Gratitude, skepticism, grief, forgiveness, caution, and disagreement do not become saved relationship values.

## 16. Passage-selection map

**Use a scene** when a verified reader-facing owner can stage a present response without claiming a sourced location. **Use a record** when existing content supports authorship and provenance. **Use a conversation** when a second perspective adds a genuine question rather than repeating the thesis. **Use a return vignette** only if the existing route already supports the callback condition; otherwise use its non-conditional final line or omit it. These are writing choices, not feature requests.

## 17. Content boundaries and open questions

| Topic | Current evidence | Editorial limit |
|---|---|---|
| Anchor | memorial_meridian_compact in the memorial catalog | Presence does not prove a live route or consumer. |
| Public wording | A friend from the Meridian Compact who stood with us in our darkest hour. Their alliance brought us hope when we had none. We will not forget. | Preserve exact text and separate it from interpretation. |
| Proposed voices | Anonymous roles in this document | Do not claim they are witnesses or canonical characters. |
| Reader response | Four prose alternatives per beat | No new menu, flag, score, reward, relationship, or save behavior. |
| Sensitive subject | ally and tags ['alliance', 'friendship', 'gratitude'] | No operational guidance, diagnosis, guarantee, or unsupported outcome. |
